      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.356.635.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2004/0/267/#laws.10.2.0">2004 c 267 art 10 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/169/#laws.4.45.0">2009 c 169 art 4 s 45</a>,46; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2012/0/286/#laws.9.6.0">2012 c 286 art 9 s 6</a>,7; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2013/0/111/#laws.3.30.0">2013 c 111 art 3 s 30</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/296/#laws.13.20.0">2014 c 296 art 13 s 20</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2015/0/68/#laws.12.40.0">2015 c 68 art 12 s 40</a>,41; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2018/0/211/#laws.13.3.0">2018 c 211 art 13 s 3</a>-7; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2021/0/22/#laws.10.2.0">2021 c 22 art 10 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/102/#laws.8.8.0">2024 c 102 art 8 s 8</a>-11</p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.356.636">
        <h1 class="shn">356.636 CORRECTION OF ERRORS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.356.636.1">
          <a title="Link to Subdivision 1." class="permalink" href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/2024/cite/356/full#stat.356.636.1">§</a>
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Definitions.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) For purposes of this section, the following terms have the meanings given.</p>
          <p>(b) "Pension fund" means the Minnesota State Retirement System, the Public Employees Retirement Association, the Teachers Retirement Association, and the St. Paul Teachers Retirement Fund Association.</p>
          <p>(c) "Tax qualification" means compliance with all applicable requirements of section 401(a) or 457(b) of the Internal Revenue Code.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.356.636.2">
          <a title="Link to Subd. 2." class="permalink" href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/2024/cite/356/full#stat.356.636.2">§</a>
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Correction of errors.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The executive director of a pension fund may correct an operational, demographic, or employer or employee eligibility error, or an error in a plan document that is not a statute if the executive director determines that correction is necessary or appropriate to preserve and protect the tax qualification of any pension or retirement plan listed in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/356.611#stat.356.611.6">356.611, subdivision 6</a>, that is part of the pension fund. The method of correction must comply with the Internal Revenue Service Employee Plans Compliance Resolution System (EPCRS) or any successor thereto, if the EPCRS addresses the error and correction.</p>
          <p>(b) To the extent deemed necessary by the executive director to implement correction, the executive director may:</p>
          <p>(1) make distributions;</p>
          <p>(2) transfer assets;</p>
          <p>(3) recover an overpayment by reducing future benefit payments or designating appropriate revenue or source of funding that will restore to the plan the amount of the overpayment; or</p>
          <p>(4) take any other action that will restore the plan and any affected member or participant to the position the plan, member, or participant would have been in had the error not occurred.</p>
          <p>(c) An executive director may correct an error under paragraph (a) or (b) without regard to any statute that imposes a time limitation on making such correction.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.356.636.3">
          <a title="Link to Subd. 3." class="permalink" href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/2024/cite/356/full#stat.356.636.3">§</a>
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Annual report.</span></h2>
          <p>The executive director of each pension fund must report annually, no later than each February 1, to the chair and executive director of the Legislative Commission on Pensions and Retirement on whether the executive director of the pension fund corrected any operational, demographic, employer or employee eligibility, or plan document error during the preceding calendar year. The report must describe the error, the pension or retirement plan affected by the error, the method of correction, and the cost, if any, to the pension or retirement plan, employee, or employer of the error and correction.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.356.636.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/102/#laws.8.12.0">2024 c 102 art 8 s 12</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.356.637">
        <h1 class="shn">356.637 CORRECTION OF ERRONEOUS DEFINED BENEFIT PLAN COVERAGE.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.356.637.1">
          <a title="Link to Subdivision 1." class="permalink" href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/2024/cite/356/full#stat.356.637.1">§</a>
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Definitions.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) For purposes of this section, the terms in paragraphs (b) to (e) have the meanings given them.</p>
          <p>(b) "Chief administrative officer" means the person selected or elected by the governing board of a covered pension plan with primary responsibility to administer the covered pension plan, or that person's designee or representative.</p>
          <p>(c) "Covered pension plan" means a plan enumerated in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/356.30#stat.356.30.3">356.30, subdivision 3</a>, except clauses (3) and (5).</p>
          <p>(d) "Governing board" means the governing board of the Minnesota State Retirement System, the Public Employees Retirement Association, the Teachers Retirement Association, or the St. Paul Teachers Retirement Fund Association.</p>
          <p>(e) "Member" means an active plan member in a covered pension plan.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.356.637.2">
          <a title="Link to Subd. 2." class="permalink" href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/2024/cite/356/full#stat.356.637.2">§</a>
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Treatment of terminated employee coverage error.</span></h2>
          <p>Any person who terminated the erroneously covered service before a chief administrative officer determined the covered pension plan coverage was in error retains the coverage with the plan that originally credited the service.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.356.637.3">
          <a title="Link to Subd. 3." class="permalink" href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/2024/cite/356/full#stat.356.637.3">§</a>
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Active employee correction of prospective service coverage.</span></h2>
          <p>Upon determination by a chief administrative officer that a member is covered by the wrong pension plan, the employer must stop remitting the erroneous employee deductions and employer contributions and report the employee to the correct covered pension plan for all subsequent service.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.356.637.4">
          <a title="Link to Subd. 4." class="permalink" href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/2024/cite/356/full#stat.356.637.4">§</a>
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Active employee treatment of past service.</span></h2>
          <p>Any plan member, with past service credited in an erroneous plan, retains the coverage for that past service with the plan that originally credited that service if the reporting error began earlier than two fiscal years prior to the current fiscal year in which the error was determined by the chief administrative officer. If the reporting error began within two fiscal years prior to the current fiscal year, the pension plan coverage for that past service must be corrected as provided in subdivision 5.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.356.637.5">
          <a title="Link to Subd. 5." class="permalink" href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/2024/cite/356/full#stat.356.637.5">§</a>
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Past service transfer procedure.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) For cases under subdivision 4 requiring correction of prior service coverage, on behalf of the applicable member the chief administrative officer of the covered pension plan fund that has received erroneous employee deductions and employer contributions must transfer to the appropriate covered retirement plan fund an amount which is the lesser of all contributions made by or on behalf of the member for the period of erroneous membership, or the specific amount requested by the chief administrative officer of the other covered pension plan which represents the employee deductions and employer contributions that would have been made had the member been properly reported.</p>
          <p>(b) If excess employee deductions remain in the member's account after the transfer of funds, the remaining erroneous amount must be refunded to the person with interest at the rate provided under the general refund law of the applicable covered pension plan. The chief administrative officer must also return any remaining excess employer contributions by providing to the employer a credit against future contributions payable by that employer.</p>
          <p>(c) If the contributions transferred to the correct covered pension plan fund are less than the amounts required for the period being corrected, the chief administrative officer of the correct covered pension plan fund must collect the remaining employee deductions and employer contributions from the employer under laws for recovering deficient contributions applicable to the correct covered pension plan, except that no interest is chargeable if the additional amounts due under this paragraph are received by the chief administrative officer within 30 days of notifying the employer of the amount due.</p>
          <p>(d) A potential transfer under this section that would cause a plan to fail to be a qualified plan under section 401(a) of the Internal Revenue Code, as amended, must not be made. Within 30 days after being notified by a chief administrative officer of an unmade potential transfer under this section, the employer of the member must transmit an amount representing the applicable salary deductions and employer contributions, without interest, to the fund of the appropriate covered pension plan. The chief administrative officer of the covered pension plan which erroneously provided coverage must provide to the employer a credit for the amount of the erroneous salary deductions and employer contributions against future contributions from that employer.</p>
          <p>(e) Upon transfer of the required assets, or payment from the employer under paragraph (d), whichever is applicable, allowable service and salary credit for the period being transferred is forfeited in the erroneous plan and is granted in the correct plan.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.356.637.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2010/0/359/#laws.2.17.0">2010 c 359 art 2 s 17</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/275/#laws.2.15.0">2014 c 275 art 2 s 15</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/296/#laws.6.44.0">2014 c 296 art 6 s 44</a>,49; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/102/#laws.8.14.0">2024 c 102 art 8 s 14</a></p>
      </div>
      <h1 class="fgh">INVESTMENT LIMITATIONS</h1>
      <div class="section" id="stat.356.64">
        <h1 class="shn">356.64 REAL ESTATE INVESTMENTS.</h1>
        <p>(a) Notwithstanding any law to the contrary, any public pension plan whose assets are not invested by the State Board of Investment may invest its funds in Minnesota situs nonfarm real estate ownership interests or loans secured by mortgages or deeds of trust if the investment is consistent with section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/356A.04">356A.04</a>.</p>
        <p>(b) An investment otherwise authorized by this section must also comply with the requirements and limitations of section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/11A.24#stat.11A.24.6">11A.24, subdivision 6</a>.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.356.64.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2002/0/392/#laws.11.46.0">2002 c 392 art 11 s 46</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2010/0/359/#laws.12.35.0">2010 c 359 art 12 s 35</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.356.645">
        <h1 class="shn">356.645 INVESTMENT OF DEFINED CONTRIBUTION PLANS AND VOLUNTEER FIREFIGHTERS RELIEF ASSOCIATIONS.</h1>
        <p>The State Board of Investment shall determine the investments to be made available to plan participants in plans defined in sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/352.965">352.965</a>, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/352.98">352.98</a>, and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/383B.46">383B.46</a> and chapters <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/352D">352D</a> and 353D and to volunteer firefighters relief associations under chapter 424A. Investments made available to plan participants and relief associations must include at least one or more of the following:</p>
        <p>(1) shares in the Minnesota supplemental investment fund established in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/11A.17">11A.17</a>;</p>
        <p>(2) savings accounts in federally insured financial institutions;</p>
        <p>(3) life insurance contracts, fixed annuity contracts, and variable annuity contracts from companies that are subject to regulation by the commissioner of commerce;</p>
        <p>(4) investment options from open-end investment companies registered under the federal Investment Company Act of 1940, United States Code, title 15, sections 80a-1 to 80a-64;</p>
        <p>(5) investment options from a firm that is a registered investment adviser under the Investment Advisers Act of 1940, United States Code, title 15, sections 80b-1 to 80b-21; and</p>
        <p>(6) investment options of a bank as defined in United States Code, title 15, section 80b-2, subsection (a), paragraph (2), or a bank holding company as defined in the Bank Holding Company Act of 1956, United States Code, title 12, section 1841, subsection (a), paragraph (1).</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.356.645.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/296/#laws.13.21.0">2014 c 296 art 13 s 21</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2018/0/211/#laws.15.8.0">2018 c 211 art 15 s 8</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2022/0/65/#laws.9.12.0">2022 c 65 art 9 s 12</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.356.646">
        <h1 class="shn">356.646 PLAN PARTICIPANT INVESTMENT RESPONSIBILITY.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.356.646.1">
          <a title="Link to Subdivision 1." class="permalink" href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/2024/cite/356/full#stat.356.646.1">§</a>
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Member investment responsibility.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">The state, State Board of Investment and its executive director and staff, plan administrators and their staff, and participating public employers are not liable and are not responsible for any investment losses due to choices made by participants or due to qualified default investment alternatives.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.356.646.2">
          <a title="Link to Subd. 2." class="permalink" href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/2024/cite/356/full#stat.356.646.2">§</a>
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Application.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">This section applies to the:</p>
          <p class="in">(1) Minnesota state deferred compensation plan, established under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/352.965">352.965</a>;</p>
          <p class="in">(2) health care savings plan, established under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/352.98">352.98</a>;</p>
          <p class="in">(3) unclassified employees retirement program, established under chapter 352D;</p>
          <p class="in">(4) public employees defined contribution plan, established under chapter 353D;</p>
          <p class="in">(5) individual retirement account plan, established under chapter 354B;</p>
          <p class="in">(6) higher education supplemental retirement plan, established under chapter 354C; and</p>
          <p class="in">(7) Arts Board and Humanities Commission individual retirement account plan, established under chapter 354D.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.356.646.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/296/#laws.13.22.0">2014 c 296 art 13 s 22</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.356.648">
        <h1 class="shn">356.648 ADDITIONAL SOURCES OF FUNDING.</h1>
        <p>Notwithstanding any other provision of law to the contrary, in addition to all sources of funding described in Minnesota Statutes, section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/356.63">356.63</a>, any public retirement plan described in Minnesota Statutes, section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/356.63">356.63</a>, paragraph (b), is authorized to accept, at its discretion, for deposit in its fund the following:</p>
        <p>(1) gifts;</p>
        <p>(2) donations;</p>
        <p>(3) bequests; and</p>
        <p>(4) life insurance death benefits.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.356.648.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2018/0/211/#laws.17.2.0">2018 c 211 art 17 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/102/#laws.8.14.0">2024 c 102 art 8 s 14</a></p>
      </div>
      <h1 class="fgh">ABANDONED PENSION FUND AMOUNTS</h1>
      <div class="section" id="stat.356.65">
        <h1 class="shn">356.65 DISPOSITION OF ABANDONED PUBLIC PENSION FUND AMOUNTS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.356.65.1">
          <a title="Link to Subdivision 1." class="permalink" href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/2024/cite/356/full#stat.356.65.1">§</a>
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Definitions.</span></h2>
          <p>For purposes of this section, unless the context clearly indicates otherwise, each of the following terms has the meaning given to it:</p>
          <p>(a) "Public pension fund" means any public pension plan as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/356.63">356.63, paragraph (b)</a>, and any Minnesota firefighters relief association which is established under chapter 424A and governed under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/424A.091">424A.091</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/424A.096">424A.096</a>.</p>
          <p>(b) "Unclaimed public pension fund amounts" means any amounts representing accumulated member contributions, any outstanding unpaid annuity, service pension or other retirement benefit payments, including those made on warrants issued by the commissioner of management and budget, which have been issued and delivered for more than six months prior to the date of the end of the fiscal year applicable to the public pension fund, and any applicable interest to the credit of:</p>
          <p>(1) an inactive or former member of a public pension fund who is not entitled to a defined retirement annuity and who has not applied for a refund of those amounts within five years after the last member contribution was made; or</p>
          <p>(2) a deceased inactive or former member of a public pension fund if no survivor is entitled to a survivor benefit and no survivor, designated beneficiary or legal representative of the estate has applied for a refund of those amounts within five years after the date of death of the inactive or former member.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.356.65.2">
          <a title="Link to Subd. 2." class="permalink" href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/2024/cite/356/full#stat.356.65.2">§</a>
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Disposition of abandoned amounts.</span></h2>
          <p>Any unclaimed public pension fund amounts existing in any public pension fund are presumed to be abandoned, but are not subject to the provisions of sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/345.31">345.31</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/345.60">345.60</a>. Unless the benefit plan of the public pension fund specifically provides for a different disposition of unclaimed or abandoned funds or amounts, any unclaimed public pension fund amounts cancel and must be credited to the public pension fund. If the unclaimed public pension fund amount exceeds $25 and the inactive or former member again becomes a member of the applicable public pension plan or applies for a retirement annuity under section 356.30 or <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/356.311">356.311</a>, whichever applies, the canceled amount must be restored to the credit of the person.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.356.65.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1981/0/224/#laws.0.178.0">1981 c 224 s 178</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1983/0/286/#laws.0.17.0">1983 c 286 s 17</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1992/0/513/#laws.4.41.0">1992 c 513 art 4 s 41</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2001/0/7/#laws.0.67.0">2001 c 7 s 67</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2002/0/392/#laws.11.47.0">2002 c 392 art 11 s 47</a>,48; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2008/0/277/#laws.1.80.0">2008 c 277 art 1 s 80</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/101/#laws.2.109.0">2009 c 101 art 2 s 109</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2010/0/359/#laws.12.36.0">2010 c 359 art 12 s 36</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2013/0/111/#laws.5.80.0">2013 c 111 art 5 s 80</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2019/0/50/#laws.1.117.0">2019 c 50 art 1 s 117</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/102/#laws.2.31.0">2024 c 102 art 2 s 31</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.356.70"><b>356.70</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/0/233/#laws.1.78.0">1997 c 233 art 1 s 78</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.356.71"><b>356.71</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2002/0/392/#laws.11.53.0">2002 c 392 art 11 s 53</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.356.80"><b>356.80</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2002/0/392/#laws.11.53.0">2002 c 392 art 11 s 53</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.356.81"><b>356.81</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2002/0/392/#laws.11.53.0">2002 c 392 art 11 s 53</a>]</div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.356.82">
        <h1 class="shn">356.82 SAVINGS CLAUSE.</h1>
        <p>The intent of the legislature in sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/352.01#stat.352.01.25">352.01, subdivision 25</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/353.01#stat.353.01.37">353.01, subdivision 37</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/354.05#stat.354.05.38">354.05, subdivision 38</a>; and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/354A.011#stat.354A.011.15a">354A.011, subdivision 15a</a> is to create a normal retirement age for persons first covered by those sections after the effective date of those sections that is the same as the retirement age in the federal Social Security law, including future amendments to that law. If a court determines that the legislature may not incorporate by reference the future changes in federal Social Security law, the legislature reserves the right to amend the appropriate sections to make the normal retirement conform to the retirement age in the federal Social Security law. No person first covered by any of those sections after the effective date of those sections has a right to a normal retirement age that is less than the retirement age in the federal Social Security law.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.356.82.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/319/#laws.13.95.0">1989 c 319 art 13 s 95</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1992/0/464/#laws.1.43.0">1992 c 464 art 1 s 43</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.356.85"><b>356.85</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/280/#laws.0.1.0">1993 c 280 s 1</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.356.86"><b>356.86</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2002/0/392/#laws.11.53.0">2002 c 392 art 11 s 53</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.356.865"><b>356.865</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2002/0/392/#laws.11.53.0">2002 c 392 art 11 s 53</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.356.866"><b>356.866</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2002/0/392/#laws.11.53.0">2002 c 392 art 11 s 53</a>]</div>
      <h1 class="fgh">OPTIONAL WITHHOLDING</h1>
      <div class="section" id="stat.356.87">
        <h1 class="shn">356.87 HEALTH INSURANCE WITHHOLDING.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.356.87.1">
          <a title="Link to Subdivision 1." class="permalink" href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/2024/cite/356/full#stat.356.87.1">§</a>
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Public employees insurance program withholding.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">(a) Upon authorization of a person entitled to receive a retirement annuity, disability benefit or survivor benefit, the executive director of a public pension fund enumerated in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/356.20#stat.356.20.2">356.20, subdivision 2</a>, shall withhold health insurance premium amounts from the retirement annuity, disability benefit or survivor benefit, and shall pay the premium amounts to the public employees insurance program.</p>
          <p class="in">(b) The public employees insurance program shall reimburse a public pension fund for the administrative expense of withholding the premium amounts and shall assume liability for the failure of a public pension fund to properly withhold the premium amounts.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.356.87.2">
          <a title="Link to Subd. 2." class="permalink" href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/2024/cite/356/full#stat.356.87.2">§</a>
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Public safety retiree insurance withholding.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">(a) For purposes of this subdivision, "governing board" means the governing board or body that has been assigned the chief policy-making powers and management duties of the applicable pension plan.</p>
          <p class="in">(b) For a pension plan covered under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/356.20#stat.356.20.2">356.20, subdivision 2</a>, that provides monthly annuity payments, the governing board may direct the plan's chief administrative officer to withhold health, accident, and long-term care insurance premiums from the retirement annuity or disability benefit and to transmit the amount to an approved insurance provider specified by the eligible person. A governing board which agrees to participate may revise or revoke that decision at a later date if the board provides reasonable notice to the applicable parties.</p>
          <p class="in">(c) An eligible person is a person who:</p>
          <p class="in">(1) is a retiree or disabilitant from a participating plan;</p>
          <p class="in">(2) was a public safety officer as defined in United States Code, title 42, section 3796b;</p>
          <p class="in">(3) terminated service as a public safety officer due to disability or attainment of normal retirement age and commences receipt of an annuity without any period of deferral; and</p>
          <p class="in">(4) satisfies any other requirements to have all or a portion of the health, accident, or long-term care insurance premiums excluded from income for taxation purposes, as specified in section 845 of Public Law 109-280, the Pension Protection Act of 2006.</p>
          <p class="in">(d) An approved insurance provider is:</p>
          <p class="in">(1) any regulated, licensed insurance company;</p>
          <p class="in">(2) a fraternal or any other organization sponsoring a regulated, licensed insurance program; or</p>
          <p class="in">(3) an employer-sponsored insurance program, whether directly through the employer or a third-party administrator.</p>
          <p class="in">(e) An eligible person may elect to have the applicable plan administrator withhold and transmit the insurance amounts described in paragraph (b). The eligible person must make this election on a form prescribed by the chief administrative officer of the applicable plan.</p>
          <p class="in">(f) A pension fund and the plan fiduciaries which authorize or administer withholding of insurance premiums under this subdivision is not liable for failure to properly withhold or transmit the premium amounts.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.356.87.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/589/#laws.2.2.0">1990 c 589 art 2 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/340/#laws.0.32.0">1991 c 340 s 32</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/341/#laws.0.47.0">1991 c 341 s 47</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/465/#laws.3.55.0">1994 c 465 art 3 s 55</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/0/248/#laws.10.17.0">1995 c 248 art 10 s 17</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2002/0/392/#laws.11.49.0">2002 c 392 art 11 s 49</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2007/0/134/#laws.11.8.0">2007 c 134 art 11 s 8</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.356.88"><b>356.88</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2002/0/392/#laws.11.53.0">2002 c 392 art 11 s 53</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.356.89"><b>356.89</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2002/0/392/#laws.11.53.0">2002 c 392 art 11 s 53</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.356.90"><b>356.90</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2007/0/134/#laws.2.50.0">2007 c 134 art 2 s 50</a>]</div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.356.91">
        <h1 class="shn">356.91 VOLUNTARY MEMBERSHIP DUES DEDUCTION.</h1>
        <p class="in">(a) Upon written authorization of a person receiving an annuity from a public pension fund administered by the Minnesota State Retirement System, the Public Employees Retirement Association, or the Teachers Retirement Association, the executive director of the public pension fund shall deduct from the retirement annuity an amount requested by the annuitant to be paid as membership dues or other payments to any labor organization that is an exclusive bargaining agent representing public employees or an organization representing retired public employees of which the annuitant is a member and shall, on a monthly basis, pay the amount to the organization so designated by the annuitant.</p>
        <p class="in">(b) A pension fund and the plan fiduciaries which authorize or administer deductions of dues payments under paragraph (a) are not liable for failure to properly deduct or transmit the dues amounts, provided that the fund and the fiduciaries have acted in good faith.</p>
        <p class="in">(c) Any labor organization that is an exclusive bargaining agent representing public employees or an organization representing retired public employees may conduct blind mailings to the annuitants of a retirement system specified in paragraph (a) by requesting that the retirement system mail voluntary membership information and dues deduction cards to annuitants. Such mailings shall not be for the purpose of supporting or opposing any candidate, political party, or ballot measure. The organization requesting the blind mailing shall pay all costs associated with these mailings, including but not limited to copying, labeling, mailing, postage, and record keeping. In lieu of administering a blind mailing in-house, a retirement system may transmit annuitant data necessary for conducting a blind mailing to a mail center pursuant to a secure data share agreement with the mail center which provides that neither the organization nor any other entity shall have direct access to the data transmitted by the retirement system. The retirement system shall have no obligation to approve or disapprove, or otherwise be responsible for, the content of the mailings. No organization shall conduct more than two blind mailings per calendar year.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.356.91.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2008/0/349/#laws.15.1.0">2008 c 349 art 15 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2010/0/359/#laws.12.37.0">2010 c 359 art 12 s 37</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2013/0/111/#laws.15.1.0">2013 c 111 art 15 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/296/#laws.13.23.0">2014 c 296 art 13 s 23</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.356.95"><b>356.95</b> MS 2008 [Expired, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2007/0/134/#laws.2.45.0">2007 c 134 art 2 s 45</a>]</div>
      <h1 class="fgh">PENSION PLAN APPEAL PROCEDURES</h1>
      <div class="section" id="stat.356.96">
        <h1 class="shn">356.96 PENSION PLAN APPEAL PROCEDURES.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.356.96.1">
          <a title="Link to Subdivision 1." class="permalink" href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/2024/cite/356/full#stat.356.96.1">§</a>
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Definitions.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">(a) Unless the language or context clearly indicates that a different meaning is intended, for the purpose of this section, the terms in this subdivision have the meanings given them.</p>
          <p class="in">(b) "Executive director" means the executive director of a covered pension plan or the executive director's designee or representative.</p>
          <p class="in">(c) "Covered pension plan" means a plan enumerated in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/356.20#stat.356.20.2">356.20, subdivision 2</a>, clauses (1) to (4), (8), and (11) to (14), but does not mean the deferred compensation plan administered under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/352.965">352.965</a> and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/352.97">352.97</a> or to the postretirement health care savings plan administered under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/352.98">352.98</a>.</p>
          <p class="in">(d) "Governing board" means the Board of Trustees of the Public Employees Retirement Association, the Board of Trustees of the Teachers Retirement Association, or the Board of Directors of the Minnesota State Retirement System.</p>
          <p class="in">(e) "Person" means an active, retired, deferred, or nonvested inactive participant in a covered pension plan or a beneficiary of a participant, or an individual who has applied to be a participant or who is or may be a survivor of a participant, or the representative of a state agency or other governmental unit that employs active participants in a covered pension plan.</p>
          <p>(f) "Petitioner" means a person who has filed a petition for review of an executive director's determination under this section.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.356.96.2">
          <a title="Link to Subd. 2." class="permalink" href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/2024/cite/356/full#stat.356.96.2">§</a>
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Right to appeal to executive director; determination.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">A person may appeal a decision by the staff of a covered pension plan regarding the person's eligibility, benefits, or other rights under the plan to the executive director of the plan. The appeal must be in writing and be delivered to the executive director no later than 60 days after the date of the written notice of the staff decision. The executive director may overturn, modify, or affirm the staff's decision. The executive director's determination is subject to review under this section.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.356.96.3">
          <a title="Link to Subd. 3." class="permalink" href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/2024/cite/356/full#stat.356.96.3">§</a>
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Notice of determination.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">The executive director shall issue a written notice of determination to the person who files an appeal under subdivision 2. The notice of determination must be delivered by certified mail to the address to which the most recent benefit payment was sent or, if that address is that of a financial institution, to the last known address of the person. The notice of determination shall include the following:</p>
          <p class="in">(1) a statement of the reasons for the determination;</p>
          <p class="in">(2) a statement that the person may petition the governing board of the covered pension plan for a review of the determination and that a person's petition for review must be filed in the administrative office of the covered pension plan no later than 60 days after the date of the written notice of determination;</p>
          <p class="in">(3) a statement indicating that a failure to petition for review within 60 days precludes the person from any further administrative or judicial review of the executive director's determination;</p>
          <p class="in">(4) a statement indicating that all relevant materials, documents, affidavits, and other records that the person wishes to be reviewed in support of the petition and a list of any witnesses who will testify before the governing board, along with a summary of the witness' testimony, must be filed with the administrative office of the covered pension plan at least 15 days before the date of the hearing under subdivision 10 or as directed by the administrative law judge who conducts a fact-finding conference under subdivision 7, paragraph (b), or a contested case hearing under subdivision 12, paragraph (b);</p>
          <p class="in">(5) a summary of this section, including all filing requirements and deadlines; and</p>
          <p>(6) the statement required under subdivision 4, paragraph (a), if applicable.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.356.96.4">
          <a title="Link to Subd. 4." class="permalink" href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/2024/cite/356/full#stat.356.96.4">§</a>
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Termination of benefits.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">(a) If the executive director's determination will terminate a benefit that is being paid to a person, the notice of determination must also state that the person has the opportunity to explain, in writing, in person, by telephone, or by email, the reasons that the benefit should not be terminated.</p>
          <p class="in">(b) If the notice of determination is returned as undeliverable and the person cannot be reached by any other reasonable means of communication and the executive director determines that a failure to terminate the benefit will result in unauthorized payment by a covered pension plan, the executive director may terminate the benefit immediately.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.356.96.5">
          <a title="Link to Subd. 5." class="permalink" href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/2024/cite/356/full#stat.356.96.5">§</a>
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Petition for review.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">(a) Upon receipt of the notice of determination required in subdivision 3, a person may petition the governing board of the covered pension plan for a review of the executive director's determination.</p>
          <p class="in">(b) The petitioner must file the petition for review with the administrative office of the covered pension plan no later than 60 days after the date of the notice of determination required by subdivision 3. Filing of the petition is effective upon mailing or personal delivery. The petition must include the petitioner's statement of the reason or reasons that the determination of the executive director should be reversed or modified.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.356.96.6">
          <a title="Link to Subd. 6." class="permalink" href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/2024/cite/356/full#stat.356.96.6">§</a>
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Failure to petition.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">If a timely petition for review under subdivision 5 is not filed with the administrative office of the covered pension plan, the executive director's determination is final and is not subject to further administrative or judicial review.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.356.96.7">
          <a title="Link to Subd. 7." class="permalink" href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/2024/cite/356/full#stat.356.96.7">§</a>
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.<span class="headnote">Notice of hearing; fact-finding; filing and timing requirements.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">(a) After receiving a petition, the executive director must schedule a hearing to review the petition before the governing board of the covered pension plan or the executive director may defer the scheduling of a hearing until after a fact-finding conference under paragraph (b).</p>
          <p class="in">(b) The executive director may direct the petitioner to participate in a fact-finding conference conducted by an administrative law judge assigned by the Office of Administrative Hearings. The fact-finding conference is an informal proceeding not subject to Minnesota Rules, chapter <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/rules/1400">1400</a>, except that Minnesota Rules, part <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/rules/1400.7300">1400.7300</a>, shall govern the admissibility of evidence and Minnesota Rules, part <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/rules/1400.8603">1400.8603</a>, shall govern how the fact-finding conference is conducted. The administrative law judge must issue a report and a recommendation to the governing board.</p>
          <p class="in">(c) If the petitioner's claim relates to disability benefits, the executive director may direct the petitioner to participate in a vocational assessment conducted by a qualified rehabilitation counselor under contract with the covered pension plan. The counselor must issue a report regarding the assessment to the governing board.</p>
          <p class="in">(d) Not less than 30 calendar days before the date scheduled for the hearing before the governing board, the executive director must notify the petitioner of the time and place of the meeting at which the governing board is scheduled to conduct the hearing. If there has been no fact-finding conference under paragraph (b), not less than 15 days before the date scheduled for the hearing, the petitioner and the executive director must provide to the governing board and the other party copies of all documentary evidence that will be presented and a list of witnesses who will testify, along with a summary of their testimony.</p>
          <p class="in">(e) The petitioner may request a postponement of the date scheduled for the hearing before the governing board within a reasonable time, but no later than ten calendar days before the scheduled hearing date. A petitioner shall be granted only one postponement unless the applicable covered pension plan agrees to additional postponements.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.356.96.8">
          <a title="Link to Subd. 8." class="permalink" href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/2024/cite/356/full#stat.356.96.8">§</a>
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 8.<span class="headnote">Record for review.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">(a) All evidence, including all records, documents, and affidavits in the possession of the covered pension plan of which the covered pension plan desires to avail itself and be considered by the governing board, and all evidence which the petitioner wishes to present to the governing board, including any evidence which would otherwise be classified by law as "private," must be made part of the hearing record.</p>
          <p class="in">(b) The executive director must provide a copy of the record to each member of the governing board at least five days before the scheduled hearing date.</p>
          <p class="in">(c) If a fact-finding conference under subdivision 7, paragraph (b), is not conducted, the record is limited to those materials provided to the petitioner in accordance with subdivision 7, paragraph (d), those filed by the petitioner with the covered pension plan in a timely manner in accordance with subdivision 7, paragraph (e), any vocational assessment report under subdivision 7, paragraph (c), and any testimony at the hearing before the governing board. Any additional evidence may be placed in the record pursuant to subdivision 10, paragraph (b).</p>
          <p>(d) If a fact-finding conference under subdivision 7, paragraph (b), or a contested case hearing under subdivision 12, paragraph (b), is conducted, the record before the governing board must be limited to the following:</p>
          <p>(1) the record from the Office of Administrative Hearings;</p>
          <p>(2) seven-page submissions by the petitioner and a representative of the covered pension plan commenting on the administrative law judge's recommendation; and</p>
          <p>(3) any vocational assessment report under subdivision 7, paragraph (c).</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.356.96.9">
          <a title="Link to Subd. 9." class="permalink" href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/2024/cite/356/full#stat.356.96.9">§</a>
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 9.<span class="headnote">Amended determination.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">At any time before the hearing before the governing board, for good cause shown and made part of the records of the plan, the executive director may reverse, alter, amend, or modify the prior decision which is subject to review under this section by issuing an amended determination to the petitioner. Upon doing so, the executive director may cancel the governing board's scheduled review of the person's petition and notify the petitioner.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.356.96.10">
          <a title="Link to Subd. 10." class="permalink" href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/2024/cite/356/full#stat.356.96.10">§</a>
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 10.<span class="headnote">Board hearing.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">(a) The governing board shall hold a timely hearing on a petition for review as part of a regularly scheduled board meeting, or as part of a special meeting if so scheduled. All governing board members who participate in the decision-making process must be familiar with the record. The governing board shall make its decision on a petition solely on the record as submitted and on the proceedings of the hearing.</p>
          <p class="in">(b) At the hearing, the petitioner, the petitioner's representative, if any, the executive director, and a representative of the covered pension plan who does not also serve as the governing board's legal advisor during the board's decision-making process may state and discuss with the governing board their positions with respect to the petition. If no fact-finding conference under subdivision 7, paragraph (b), or contested case hearing under subdivision 12, paragraph (b), was conducted, additional evidence may be received in the form of testimony from previously disclosed witnesses. The governing board may allow further documentation to be placed in the record at the board meeting only with the agreement of both the executive director and the petitioner. The executive director may not otherwise participate in the board's decision-making process.</p>
          <p class="in">(c) If a petitioner who received timely notice of a scheduled hearing fails to appear, the governing board may nevertheless hear the petition and issue a decision.</p>
          <p>(d) The governing board's decision shall be made upon a motion by a board member and approval by a majority of the governing board. The governing board must issue its decision as a written order containing findings of fact, conclusions of law, and the board's decision no later than 30 days after the hearing. If the decision is contrary to the petitioner's desired outcome, the notice must inform the petitioner of the appeal rights set forth in subdivision 13.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.356.96.11">
          <a title="Link to Subd. 11." class="permalink" href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/2024/cite/356/full#stat.356.96.11">§</a>
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 11.<span class="headnote">Disability medical issues.</span></h2>
          <p class="in"> If the petitioner seeks to reverse or modify a determination by the executive director that there was insufficient medical data to support an application for disability benefits, the governing board may reverse that determination only if there is medical evidence supporting the application. The governing board has the discretion to resubmit a disability benefit application at any time to a medical advisor for reconsideration, and the resubmission may include an instruction that further medical examinations be obtained.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.356.96.12">
          <a title="Link to Subd. 12." class="permalink" href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/2024/cite/356/full#stat.356.96.12">§</a>
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 12.<span class="headnote">Referral for administrative hearing.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">(a) A fact-finding conference under subdivision 7, paragraph (b), must be conducted exclusively under the procedures set forth in this section and not as a contested case under chapter <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/14">14</a>.</p>
          <p class="in">(b) A governing board, in its sole discretion, may refer a petition brought under this section to the Office of Administrative Hearings for a contested case hearing under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/14.57">14.57</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/14.69">14.69</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.356.96.13">
          <a title="Link to Subd. 13." class="permalink" href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/2024/cite/356/full#stat.356.96.13">§</a>
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 13.<span class="headnote">Appeal of the governing board's decision; judicial review.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">No later than 60 days after the date of the mailing of the notice of the governing board's decision, the petitioner may appeal the decision by filing a writ of certiorari with the court of appeals under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/606.01">606.01</a> and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/court_rules/ap/subtype/rcap/id/115/">Rule 115</a> of the Minnesota Rules of Civil Appellate Procedure. Failure by a person to appeal to the court of appeals within the 60-day period precludes the person from later raising, in any subsequent administrative hearing or court proceeding, those substantive and procedural issues that reasonably should have been raised upon a timely appeal.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.356.96.14">
          <a title="Link to Subd. 14." class="permalink" href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/2024/cite/356/full#stat.356.96.14">§</a>
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 14.</h2>
          <p>MS 2016 [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2018/0/211/#laws.13.21.0">2018 c 211 art 13 s 21</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.356.96.15">
          <a title="Link to Subd. 15." class="permalink" href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/2024/cite/356/full#stat.356.96.15">§</a>
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 15.</h2>
          <p>MS 2016 [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2018/0/211/#laws.13.21.0">2018 c 211 art 13 s 21</a>]</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.356.96.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2007/0/134/#laws.6.2.0">2007 c 134 art 6 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2008/0/349/#laws.10.16.0">2008 c 349 art 10 s 16</a>; art 11 s 7; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/169/#laws.4.47.0">2009 c 169 art 4 s 47</a>; art 9 s 28; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2010/0/359/#laws.5.20.0">2010 c 359 art 5 s 20</a>-24; art 12 s 38; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/275/#laws.2.25.0">2014 c 275 art 2 s 25</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2018/0/211/#laws.13.8.0">2018 c 211 art 13 s 8</a>-20</p>
      </div>
      <h1 class="fgh">PLAN ADMINISTRATION</h1>
      <div class="section" id="stat.356.97">
        <h1 class="shn">356.97 INFORMATION SERVICES AND TECHNOLOGY.</h1>
        <p>The executive directors subject to the standard of fiduciary conduct under chapter 356A and the boards to which they report are exempt from the requirements of chapter 16E. An executive director or a board may use services provided by the Department of Information Technology Services.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.356.97.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2019/1/8/#laws.6.3.0">1Sp2019 c 8 art 6 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2021/0/31/#laws.2.16.0">2021 c 31 art 2 s 16</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.356.98">
        <h1 class="shn">356.98 ALLOCATION OF RECEIVABLES.</h1>
        <p class="in">If an employing unit is dissolved or closed and amounts are owed to more than one Minnesota public pension plan, any amounts available to cover payments to the plans must be applied first to the employee contributions owed to the applicable plans, and next to the unpaid employer contributions, including any applicable employer additional contributions, and finally to the interest due on the employee and employer amounts. If, at any stage in this allocation process, the available amount is insufficient to fully cover the amount required, the remaining available payment amount must be prorated among the applicable plans based on each plan's share of combined covered payroll.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.356.98.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2007/0/134/#laws.2.46.0">2007 c 134 art 2 s 46</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.356.99"><b>356.99</b> [Renumbered <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/356.637">356.637</a>]</div>
    </div>
